## 2<sub>.</sub> R<sub>e</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d W<sub>or</sub>k

Continuous language difusion and flow. Difusion-LM and self-conditioned embedding difusion established continuous denoisin<sub>g</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>roaches to lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e <sub>g</sub>eneration (Li et al., 2022; Strudel et al., 2022), while CDCD and Plaid develo<sub>p</sub>ed cate<sub>g</sub>orical difusion and likelihood-based trainin<sub>g</sub> (Dieleman et al., 2022; Gulrajani and Hashimoto, 2023). Recent work ex<sub>p</sub>lores flow ma<sub>p</sub>s (Lee et al., 2026), learned embeddin<sub>g</sub>s with information-based noise schedulin<sub>g</sub> (Chen et al., 2026), architectural refinements (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026), h<sub>yp</sub>ers<sub>p</sub>herical d<sub>y</sub>namics (Deschenaux and Gulcehre, 2026), and embeddin<sub>g</sub>-s<sub>p</sub>ace flow matchin<sub>g</sub> (Hu et al., 2026). We stud<sub>y</sub> architectural desi<sub>g</sub>n within fixed L<sub>ang</sub>Fl<sub>ow an</sub>d Pl<sub>a</sub>id<sub>-s</sub>t<sub>y</sub>l<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub>

Latent representations and decoding. CoDAR uses contextual autoregressive decoding (Shen et al., 2026), while LDLM jointly learns an encoder, denoiser, and decoder with controlled gradient interactions (Meshchaninov et al., 2026). Cola-DLM introduces a text VAE and latent <sub>p</sub>rior (Guo et al., 2026), and AURORA-LM restricts the nois<sub>y</sub>-in<sub>p</sub>ut <sub>p</sub>athwa<sub>y</sub> while <sub>p</sub>reservin<sub>g</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h-ca<sub>p</sub>acit<sub>y</sub> latents (Lian<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026). Anal<sub>y</sub>sis of ELF links token recover<sub>y</sub> to decoder mar<sub>g</sub>ins and denoisin<sub>g</sub> trajectories (Du and Ma, 2026). Our chan<sub>g</sub>es o<sub>p</sub>erate within the existin<sub>g</sub> codebook-based <sub>p</sub>rediction interface.

Normalization and conditioning. DiT and EDM2 emphasize adaptive conditioning and magnitude control in difusion networks (Peebles and Xie, 2023; Karras et al., 2024). RMSNorm, nGPT, and <sub>query–</sub>k<sub>ey</sub> <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>prov</sub>id<sub>e</sub> <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>ary</sub> <sub>approac</sub>h<sub>es</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>lli<sub>ng</sub> <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d attention scales (Zhan<sub>g</sub> and Sennrich, 2019; Loshchilov et al., 2025; Henr<sub>y</sub> et al., 2020). Related work examines attention-entro<sub>py</sub> colla<sub>p</sub>se (Zhai et al., 2023) and uses <sub>q</sub>uer<sub>y</sub>–ke<sub>y</sub> RMSNorm in rectified-flow Transformers (Esser et al., 2024). We examine these normalization choices alon<sub>g</sub>side <sub>no</sub>i<sub>se-con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ng capac</sub>it<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n con</sub>ti<sub>nuous</sub> l<sub>anguage</sub> d<sub>eno</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub>

Self-conditioning. Analog Bits, self-conditioned embedding difusion, and LangFlow use previous <sub>p</sub>redictions to <sub>g</sub>uide denoisin<sub>g</sub> (Chen et al., 2022; Strudel et al., 2022; Chen et al., 2026). AURORA-LM addresses trainin<sub>g</sub>–inference mismatch throu<sub>g</sub>h trajector<sub>y</sub> consistenc<sub>y</sub> (Lian<sub>g</sub> et al., 2026). Our <sub>en</sub>t<sub>ropy-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>ga</sub>t<sub>e regu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> b<sub>oo</sub>t<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rap</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t<sub>s</sub> d<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng w</sub>hil<sub>e re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> l<sub>oss</sub> f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence samp</sub>l<sub>er.</sub>

Discrete difusion and evaluation. MDLM, LLaDA, and iLLaDA advance masked difusion language modelin<sub>g</sub> (Sahoo et al., 2024; Nie et al., 2025, 2026), while Duo connects uniform-state discrete difusion to an underl<sub>y</sub>in<sub>g</sub> Gaussian <sub>p</sub>rocess (Sahoo et al., 2025). Across difusion families, likelihood rankin<sub>g</sub>s can difer from those based on <sub>g</sub>eneration <sub>q</sub>ualit<sub>y</sub> and sam<sub>p</sub>lin<sub>g</sub> cost (Sahoo et al., 2026); lower GenPPL can also accom<sub>p</sub>an<sub>y</sub> reduced distributional covera<sub>g</sub>e (Franca and Ton<sub>g</sub>, 2026). We th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore</sub> <sub>repor</sub>t G<sub>en</sub>PPL <sub>a</sub>l<sub>ongs</sub>id<sub>e</sub> <sub>mean</sub> <sub>per-samp</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>un</sub>i<sub>gram</sub> <sub>en</sub>t<sub>ropy,</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>exp</sub>li<sub>c</sub>it <sub>samp</sub>li<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s.</sub>

<table><tr><td>Component</td><td>Design principle</td><td>Implementation</td></tr><tr><td>Backbone</td><td>Separate feature-scale control from noise-dependent modulation.</td><td>Backbone and Q/K RMSNorm; wider noise conditioning.</td></tr><tr><td>Vocabulary head</td><td>Control the shortcut&#x27;s forward contribution and direct codebook gradients separately.</td><td>Shortcut detachment; bounded learned calibration.</td></tr><tr><td>Self-conditioning</td><td>Distinguish clean-state estimation from auxiliary feedback during training.</td><td>Confidence-gated bootstrap inputs; unweighted posterior-mean estimates at inference.</td></tr></table>

## 3.2. Continuous Representation and Training Objective

L<sub>ang</sub>Fl<sub>ow</sub> <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith l<sub>earne</sub>d <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ngs</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>genera</sub>t<sub>es</sub> l<sub>anguage</sub> b<sub>y</sub> t<sub>ranspor</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> Gaussian noise toward these embeddin<sub>g</sub>s (Chen et al., 2026). Its denoiser <sub>p</sub>redicts token <sub>p</sub>robabilities, <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ose</sub> <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng</sub> <sub>mean</sub> <sub>supp</sub>li<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>con</sub>ti<sub>nuous</sub> <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fl<sub>ow.</sub>

Clean embeddings and noisy inputs. Let V denote the vocabulary, $y _ { 1 : L } \in \mathcal { V } ^ { L }$ <sub>a</sub> t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en</sub> <sub>sequence,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d $E \in \mathbb { R } ^ { | \mathcal { V } | \times d }$ <sub>a</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d <sub>co</sub>d<sub>e</sub>b<sub>oo</sub>k<sub>.</sub> E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>row</sub> i<sub>s norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d t<sub>o norm</sub> � <sub>an</sub>d <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> $s = 0 . 1$

$$
\widetilde { E } _ { \nu } = \sqrt { d } \frac { E _ { \nu } } { \| E _ { \nu } \| _ { 2 } } , \qquad C _ { \nu } = s \widetilde { E } _ { \nu } , \qquad \nu \in \mathcal { V } .\tag{1}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ean-em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> $C = s \widetilde { E }$ . T<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>uses</sub> t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>cross-en</sub>t<sub>ropy:</sub>

$$
\mathcal { L } _ { \mathrm { C E } } = \mathbb { E } _ { y _ { 1 : L } \sim p _ { \mathrm { d a t a } } , \gamma \sim \pi , \epsilon } \left[ - \frac { 1 } { L } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { L } \log p _ { \theta , i } ( y _ { i } \mid z _ { \gamma } , \gamma ) \right] .\tag{3}
$$

Here, $p _ { \mathrm { d a t a } }$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>sequence</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d $\pi$ i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d G<sub>um</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>over</sub> <sub>no</sub>i<sub>se</sub> levels used b<sub>y</sub> Lan<sub>g</sub>Flow’s information-based scheduler (Chen et al., 2026). All variants retain this loss formulation and the baseline entropy-matching objective for the schedule.

Th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>kb<sub>one processes no</sub>i<sub>sy em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ngs w</sub>hil<sub>e a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e no</sub>i<sub>se</sub> l<sub>eve</sub>l<sub>.</sub> W<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>u</sub>d<sub>y norma</sub>li<sub>za-</sub> ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>no</sub>i<sub>se con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ng as separa</sub>t<sub>e componen</sub>t<sub>s: norma</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>s</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ure sca</sub>l<sub>es, w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ng supp</sub>li<sub>es no</sub>i<sub>se-</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>t <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Input and self-conditioning fusion. The input state is

$$
h _ { 0 } = z _ { \gamma } + P ( [ z _ { \gamma } ; x _ { \mathrm { S C } } ] ) ,\tag{4}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> b<sub>rac</sub>k<sub>e</sub>t<sub>s</sub> d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e conca</sub>t<sub>ena</sub>ti<sub>on a</sub>l<sub>ong</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>on an</sub>d ${ \cal P } : \mathbb { R } ^ { 2 d }  \mathbb { R } ^ { d }$ i<sub>s a zero-</sub> initialized, bias-free linear projection applied independently at each position. The embedding and b<sub>ac</sub>kb<sub>one</sub> <sub>w</sub>idth<sub>s</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>equa</sub>l i<sub>n</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure.</sub> Wh<sub>en</sub> <sub>se</sub>lf<sub>-con</sub>diti<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub>b<sub>sen</sub>t<sub>,</sub> $x _ { S C } = 0$

Transformer blocks. Each block contains bidirectional attention and a feed-forward sublayer, each with a noise-conditioned residual <sub>g</sub>ate. Followin<sub>g</sub> DiT (Peebles and Xie, 2023), a noise embeddin<sub>g</sub> $c _ { \gamma }$ <sub>supp</sub>li<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>hift<sub>s,</sub> <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>es,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>ga</sub>t<sub>es:</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r l } & { u = h + g _ { a } ( c _ { \gamma } ) \odot \mathrm { A t t n } \big ( \mathrm { m o d } _ { a } ( N _ { a } ( h ) ; c _ { \gamma } ) \big ) , } \\ & { h ^ { \prime } = u + g _ { m } ( c _ { \gamma } ) \odot \mathrm { M L P } \big ( \mathrm { m o d } _ { m } ( N _ { m } ( u ) ; c _ { \gamma } ) \big ) . } \end{array}\tag{5}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>su</sub>b<sub>scr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>�</sub> <sub>an</sub>d $m$ id<sub>en</sub>tif<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>an</sub>d MLP <sub>su</sub>bl<sub>ayers;</sub> bl<sub>oc</sub>k i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ces</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>om</sub>itt<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> <sub>rea</sub>d<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y.</sub> W<sub>e wr</sub>it<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e a</sub>fi<sub>ne mo</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on as</sub>

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathrm { m o d } _ { j } ( \nu ; c _ { \gamma } ) = \big ( 1 + \delta _ { j } ( c _ { \gamma } ) \big ) \odot \nu + \beta _ { j } ( c _ { \gamma } ) , \qquad j \in \{ a , m \} , } \end{array}\tag{6}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>vec</sub>t<sub>ors</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>res</sub>id<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>ga</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>are</sub> b<sub>roa</sub>d<sub>cas</sub>t <sub>across</sub> <sub>sequence</sub> <sub>pos</sub>iti<sub>ons.</sub>

Backbone RMSNorm. LayerNorm subtracts the feature mean before normalizing magnitude. To <sub>exam</sub>i<sub>ne w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> thi<sub>s re-cen</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>s nee</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng-space</sub> d<sub>eno</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng, we rep</sub>l<sub>ace</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>pre-norma</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>ayers</sub> i<sub>n eac</sub>h T<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>ormer</sub> bl<sub>oc</sub>k <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e norma</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>ayer</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e voca</sub>b<sub>u</sub>l<sub>ary</sub> head with RMSNorm (Zhan<sub>g</sub> and Sennrich, 2019):

$$
\mathrm { R M S N o r m } ( x ) = g \odot \frac { x } { \sqrt { d ^ { - 1 } \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { d } x _ { j } ^ { 2 } + \varepsilon } } , \qquad \varepsilon = 1 0 ^ { - 6 } .\tag{7}
$$

The mean-square reduction is computed in FP32, and the output is cast to the projection dtype. Both <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> l<sub>ayers use a</sub> l<sub>earne</sub>d <sub>ga</sub>i<sub>n w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>a</sub> bi<sub>as, so</sub> th<sub>e rep</sub>l<sub>acemen</sub>t <sub>preserves</sub> th<sub>e parame</sub>t<sub>er</sub> <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>.</sub> RMSN<sub>orm resca</sub>l<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>remov</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> it<sub>s mean componen</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e a</sub>bl<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f thi<sub>s c</sub>h<sub>ange w</sub>hil<sub>e re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>ti<sub>ve mo</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Query–key RMSNorm. Normalizing the input to attention does not directly constrain the magnitudes produced by the learned query and key projections. In dot-product attention, changes in ∥�∥ ∥�∥ <sub>a</sub>lt<sub>er</sub> l<sub>og</sub>it <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>en</sub>tl<sub>y o</sub>f di<sub>rec</sub>ti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>m</sub>il<sub>ar</sub>it<sub>y.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore app</sub>l<sub>y</sub> RMSN<sub>orm</sub> t<sub>o eac</sub>h 64<sub>-</sub> dimensional query and key vector after the fused QKV projection and head reshape, before rotary <sub>pos</sub>iti<sub>on em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ngs:</sub>

$$
\widehat { q } _ { i , h } = \mathrm { R M S N o r m } ( q _ { i , h } ) , \qquad \widehat { k } _ { i , h } = \mathrm { R M S N o r m } ( k _ { i , h } ) .\tag{8}
$$

Queries and keys have separate learned gains, each shared across heads within a layer; values are unchan<sub>g</sub>ed. The desi<sub>g</sub>n follows QK normalization (Henr<sub>y</sub> et al., 2020) and its RMSNorm im<sub>p</sub>lementation in rectified-flow Transformers (Esser et al., 2024). For head dimension $d _ { h }$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>ga</sub>i<sub>n</sub> <sub>vec</sub>t<sub>ors</sub> $g _ { q } .$ , ��, th<sub>e norma</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d d<sub>o</sub>t<sub>-pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>t l<sub>og</sub>it <sub>o</sub>b<sub>eys</sub>

$$
\left| \frac { ( R _ { i } \widehat { q } ) ^ { \top } ( R _ { j } \widehat { k } ) } { \sqrt { d _ { h } } } \right| \leq \sqrt { d _ { h } } \| g _ { q } \| _ { \infty } \| g _ { k } \| _ { \infty } ,\tag{9}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $R _ { i } , R _ { j }$ <sub>are</sub> th<sub>e or</sub>th<sub>ogona</sub>l <sub>ro</sub>t<sub>ary-pos</sub>iti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>ons.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d <sub>removes raw</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t <sub>norms</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>r</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>-</sub>h<sub>an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>id<sub>e;</sub> it d<sub>oes</sub> <sub>no</sub>t fi<sub>x</sub> <sub>a</sub> t<sub>empera</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> <sub>or</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d l<sub>earne</sub>d <sub>ga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> <sub>over</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> A<sub>.</sub>1 <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>va</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Th<sub>e a</sub>bl<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons assess w</sub>h<sub>e</sub>th<sub>er</sub> thi<sub>s sca</sub>l<sub>e con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l i<sub>mproves genera</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Noise-conditioning capacity. The conditioning vector supplies shifts, scales, and gates to all 12 T<sub>rans</sub>f<sub>ormer</sub> bl<sub>oc</sub>k<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>parame</sub>t<sub>ers</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>voca</sub>b<sub>u</sub>l<sub>ary</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>d<sub>.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> i<sub>ncrease</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>w</sub>idth f<sub>rom</sub> 128 to 256, chan<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> the timeste<sub>p</sub> MLP from 256 → 128 → 128 to 256 → 256 → 256. Bounded calibration. The contextual head sees the full noisy sequence and is not constrained to model a context-only prior. We therefore allow a structured adjustment of the shortcut’s contribution:

$$
a _ { \psi } ( \gamma ) = \exp \bigl ( \mathrm { c l i p } ( f _ { \psi } ( c _ { \gamma } ) , - \log 2 , \log 2 ) \bigr ) .\tag{12}
$$

Here $f _ { \psi }$ is a 256→32→1 MLP with SiLU activation and a zero-initialized final la<sub>y</sub>er. Generation starts from Gaussian noise and proceeds from hi<sub>g</sub>h to low <sub>�</sub> usin<sub>g</sub> the baseline Euler–EDM sam<sub>p</sub>ler (Karras et al., 2022). $$

$$

## 3.7. Im<sub>p</sub>lementation Details

For OpenWebText, LangDiT uses 12 Transformer blocks with embedding and hidden width � = 768, 12 <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>e</sub>nti<sub>o</sub>n h<sub>ea</sub>d<sub>s o</sub>f dim<sub>e</sub>n<sub>s</sub>i<sub>o</sub>n 64<sub>, a</sub>nd f<sub>ee</sub>d-f<sub>o</sub>rw<sub>a</sub>rd width 3072<sub>.</sub> F<sub>o</sub>r th<sub>e</sub> L<sub>a</sub>n<sub>g</sub>Fl<sub>o</sub>w f<sub>o</sub>rm<sub>u</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> input projection � maps 1536 dimensions to 768 dimensions. Transformer depth and hidden width <sub>are re</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d f<sub>rom baseline.</sub>

Both the baseline and LangDiT use fused QKV projections, rotary position embeddings, GELU feed-forward layers, and zero-initialized adaptive-normalization projections. Data and optimization. We train from scratch on OpenWebText using Qwen3 tokenization (151,936 out<sub>p</sub>ut entries), document BOS/EOS markers, <sub>p</sub>ackin<sub>g</sub>, a 10,000-document shufle bufer, and random <sub>crops</sub> <sub>o</sub>f 1<sub>,</sub>024 t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> L<sub>ang</sub>Fl<sub>ow</sub> <sub>a</sub>bl<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>uses</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>g</sub>l<sub>o</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l b<sub>a</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>o</sub>f 512<sub>,</sub> Ad<sub>am</sub>W <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht d<sub>ecay</sub> 0.01<sub>,</sub> <sub>g</sub>radient cli<sub>pp</sub>in<sub>g</sub> at 1.0<sub>,</sub> and a 250k-u<sub>p</sub>date schedule: 5k warmu<sub>p</sub> ste<sub>p</sub>s to $3 \times 1 0 ^ { - 4 }$ f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>owe</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> <sub>cos</sub>i<sub>ne</sub> d<sub>ecay</sub> t<sub>o</sub> $3 \times 1 0 ^ { - 5 }$ .
